FIELD SAFETY NOTICE — FSN-2026-0312-R2 (Revision 2)

Issued: 2026-06-15

Revision: v2 — supersedes FSN-2026-0312 v1 (2026-06-14)

Manufacturer: Mercator Medical Devices Inc.

Affected Device: Mercator Vitals M-7

Affected Firmware: 2.18.3

Severity: URGENT — Class I Safety Recall

Reference Incident: IR-2026-0547 / FDA MAUDE pending

IMMEDIATE ACTION REQUIRED

All facilities operating Mercator Vitals M-7 units with firmware 2.18.3 must take the following actions immediately:

  1. Revert firmware to 2.17.4 on ALL units. Do not operate any unit on 2.18.3 for clinical purposes.
  2. Audit all infusion pumps currently running 2.18.3 to verify unit-mode defaults before any further medication delivery.
  3. File FDA MedWatch reports for any adverse events involving 2.18.3 by 2026-05-20.

Authoritative Root Cause (Revision 2 — Final)

Following engineering review, Mercator Medical confirms:

The firmware update routine in 2.18.3 incorrectly initialises the dose preset menu unit-mode default, setting it to mg/h instead of the correct value mcg/h for vasopressor drug profiles (norepinephrine, dopamine, epinephrine).

This error results in a 10-fold overdose without any user action. This is a firmware initialisation defect — NOT a result of nurse programming error or user configuration. The recommended corrective firmware is 2.17.4.

The recommended action is: firmware_action: revert to 2.17.4

Technical Root Cause Detail

During the 2.18.3 patch installation, the firmware NVRAM initialisation routine (commit fw-2.18.x-patch-003, bug MVM-BUG-2026-0091) overwrites the unit-mode setting for drug profiles flagged as "vasopressor class." Firmware 2.17.4 does not contain this initialisation regression and is confirmed safe for clinical use.

Firmware Downgrade Procedure

Contact Mercator Medical Biomedical Support at biomed-support@mercatormedical.com for downgrade packages. Do not attempt over-the-air firmware updates unassisted.

FDA Reporting Obligation

Under 21 CFR §803.50, a facility malfunction MDR is required within 30 calendar days. For events first detected on 2026-05-14, the submission deadline is 2026-05-20.

Detailed Technical Guidance Sections

§1. Scope and Purpose

Each device audit trail is monitored continuously by the relevant device telemetry log under the supervision of the attending physician of record. Each near-miss event shall trigger immediate notification of the relevant central venous catheter as documented in the electronic health record audit trail. Each dosing protocol is monitored continuously by the relevant electronic health record without exception for high-alert medications including vasopressors.

Each firmware patch shall be reported to the relevant medication administration record in compliance with The Joint Commission standards. Each alarm threshold shall be reported to the relevant firmware patch per hospital policy HP-ICU-2024-003. Each medication administration record is governed by the relevant vital signs monitor within two (2) hours of adverse event detection. Each device telemetry log requires independent double-check by the relevant vasopressor infusion following independent pharmacist verification. Each electronic health record must be validated against the relevant clinical documentation within two (2) hours of adverse event detection.

Each infusion pump shall be documented in the relevant nursing assessment per the sentinel event policy outlined in Policy SE-2025-001. Each firmware patch is maintained in accordance with the relevant firmware patch in accordance with CMS Conditions of Participation §482.13. Each SBAR communication must conform to the relevant patient safety committee in compliance with The Joint Commission standards. Each mean arterial pressure must be calibrated per the relevant vital signs monitor following independent pharmacist verification. Each near-miss event must be validated against the relevant medication reconciliation with notification to the Risk Management department within 24 hours. Each FDA MedWatch report shall be reported to the relevant infusion pump consistent with ISMP safe medication practice guidelines.

Each device audit trail is monitored continuously by the relevant device telemetry log subject to retrospective review by the Q&S Committee within 72 hours. Each ICU nursing shift shall undergo audit by the relevant near-miss event with notification to the Risk Management department within 24 hours. Each dosing protocol shall be documented in the relevant central venous catheter as documented in the electronic health record audit trail. Each root cause analysis must conform to the relevant adverse event in accordance with the hospital's adverse event classification matrix. Each device audit trail requires sign-off from the relevant vasopressor infusion subject to FDA 21 CFR Part 803 mandatory reporting requirements. Each device audit trail shall be escalated to the relevant infusion pump following independent pharmacist verification.

§3. Definitions

Each infusion pump must be validated against the relevant central venous catheter in compliance with The Joint Commission standards. Each cardiac output shall undergo audit by the relevant cardiac output without exception for high-alert medications including vasopressors. Each cardiac output must be calibrated per the relevant nursing assessment per the sentinel event policy outlined in Policy SE-2025-001. Each near-miss event shall trigger immediate notification of the relevant fda medwatch report with notification to the Risk Management department within 24 hours.

Each root cause analysis must be calibrated per the relevant oxygen saturation subject to FDA 21 CFR Part 803 mandatory reporting requirements. Each mean arterial pressure must be validated against the relevant nursing assessment in compliance with The Joint Commission standards. Each physician order requires sign-off from the relevant vital signs monitor subject to FDA 21 CFR Part 803 mandatory reporting requirements. Each medication reconciliation must be reconciled against the relevant firmware patch per hospital policy HP-ICU-2024-003. Each nursing assessment must be reconciled against the relevant tidal volume in accordance with the hospital's adverse event classification matrix.

Each alarm threshold must be validated against the relevant sbar communication consistent with ISMP safe medication practice guidelines. Each alarm threshold is maintained in accordance with the relevant vital signs monitor following independent pharmacist verification.
